        Idea:
Implement a professional AI animated sequence creator in the Reigh video editor. Add a Sequence tool beside the existing floating Text and Effect Layer tools. It should open a SequenceCreatorPanel for generating structured Remotion timeline sequence drafts from a user prompt plus optional selected/attached timeline assets. It should treat effects as wrappers and sequences as first-class timeline clips. It should use TimelineConfig.theme/theme_overrides when present but style/theme must be optional. It should generate trusted structured clipType+params drafts against existing registered themed sequence components first, preview them in Remotion, allow editing core params/timing/assets, then insert or replace a timeline clip. Store inserted sequences as normal timeline clips with clip.params and clip.hold; do not introduce shot-theme as source of truth. Reuse existing selected media/attachment patterns where practical, use existing timeline asset registry refs, add tests for insertion/data modeling/render path, and avoid arbitrary generated code by default.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Professional AI Sequence Creator

## Overview
The video editor already has the right foundation: `TimelineClip.clipType` is an open string, `clip.params` and `clip.hold` are persisted by `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts`, registered themed components render via `@banodoco/timeline-composition/registry.generated`, and `effect-layer` clips are already modeled as wrapper clips. The main gap is that editor row/meta conversion currently drops `params`, theme resolution is not wired into the Reigh Remotion renderer, and there is no safe prompt-to-structured-sequence flow.

The simplest implementation is to add a Sequence tool and panel that generates only trusted JSON drafts for installed registry clip types, previews those drafts by temporarily overlaying normal timeline clips, and inserts/replaces normal clips. Do not generate arbitrary React/code by default.

## Phase 1: Data Model And Render Foundation

### Step 1: Preserve sequence clip data in timeline conversion (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Extend `ClipMeta` and `PinnedShotImageClipSnapshot.meta` to carry schema-lift fields needed by sequences, especially `params`, plus any existing fields currently serialized but not represented in row/meta conversion.
2. Update `getDefaultClipMeta()` and `rowsToConfig()` so `clip.params` round-trips through editor edits, saves, undo/redo, and row rebuilds.
3. Add focused tests in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.test.ts` or a new `timeline-data` test proving a clip like `{ clipType: 'section-hook', hold: 3, params: { title: 'X' } }` survives build/edit/serialize.

### Step 2: Fix themed sequence render path (`src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Resolve `config.theme` plus `config.theme_overrides` when present, falling back to the package default theme when absent.
2. Wrap themed clip rendering in `ThemeProvider` from `@banodoco/timeline-composition/theme-api` so installed components using `useTheme()` render correctly.
3. Keep theme optional: timelines with no `theme` and no `theme_overrides` still render using default theme, and no shot-level theme becomes authoritative.
4. Add or expand `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.test.tsx` to cover a registered sequence clip rendering without placeholder and with params passed through.

## Phase 2: Trusted Sequence Drafting

### Step 3: Add a local sequence registry facade (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Create a small facade over `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY` that exposes only sequence-capable registered clip types, initial supported ids likely `section-hook`, `art-card`, `resource-card`, and `cta-card`.
2. Include editable param metadata for core fields used by existing 2rp components, for example `title`, `subtitle`, `kicker`, `caption`, `credit`, `label`, `detail`, `action`, `note`, `previews`, `entrance`, and `exit` where applicable.
3. Add validation helpers that accept only registered `clipType` values and JSON-serializable `params`; reject unknown clip types instead of falling through to generated code.

### Step 4: Add sequence generation Edge Function (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Mirror the auth, rate-limit, error, and test style from `supabase/functions/ai-generate-effect/index.ts`, but return structured JSON only.
2. Request schema: `prompt`, optional `timeline`, optional `selected_clips`, optional `allowed_clip_types`, optional `theme`, optional `theme_overrides`.
3. Response schema: `drafts: Array<{ clipType, params, hold, title?, assetRefs? }>`, optional `message`, `model`; no code fields.
4. Prompt the model with the sequence registry facade, timeline summary, and selected/attached asset refs. The model must choose existing registered clip types first and may only reference assets by timeline asset keys/URLs already supplied.
5. Add Edge tests for auth validation, bad prompt, unknown generated clip type rejection, and a valid structured draft.

## Phase 3: Editor UI And Insertion

### Step 5: Add sequence insertion helpers (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/clip-editing/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Implement pure helpers to convert a validated sequence draft into a normal timeline clip/action: `clipType`, `params`, `hold`, `track`, `at`, and optional asset refs in `params` or `asset` only when backed by the existing registry.
2. Reuse the current text/effect insertion patterns: `getNextClipId()`, `resolveOverlaps()`, `updateClipOrder()`, and `applyEdit({ type: 'rows' })`.
3. Support insert-at-playhead and replace-selected-clip. Replace should preserve selected clip `track` and `at` by default and remove the replaced clip from row/order.
4. Expose this through a hook or existing timeline ops so UI code does not duplicate row/meta mutation logic.

### Step 6: Build `SequenceCreatorPanel` (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Add a dialog/panel with prompt textarea, selected/attached media summary using `useSelectedMediaClips()` and `useTimelineClipsForAttachments()`, generate button, draft list, and an editable inspector for core params, timing, and asset refs.
2. Preview selected draft with `@remotion/player` by composing the current resolved config plus the draft clip, using existing `RemotionPreview`/`TimelineRenderer` patterns where practical.
3. Keep editing constrained to structured fields: no code editor and no arbitrary component import path.
4. Provide explicit `Insert` and `Replace selected` actions. Disable replace when no selected clip exists.

### Step 7: Add the floating Sequence tool (`src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/VideoEditorShell.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a third floating tool beside Text and Effect Layer using a lucide icon and tooltip/title.
2. Prefer click-to-open the Sequence panel over drag-first behavior; optionally allow drag later by carrying an insertion time into the panel.
3. Wire panel open state in `VideoEditorShell` or a small child component with access to timeline store contexts.
4. Preserve existing Text and Effect Layer behavior unchanged.

### Step 8: Add sequence inspector support (`src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/ClipPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. For registered sequence clips, show tabs for `timing` and a new/core params editor rather than treating them as media clips.
2. Reuse `ParameterControls` if the local sequence registry metadata maps cleanly; otherwise add a small structured params editor for strings, numbers, string arrays, and asset refs.
3. Ensure updates call `onChange({ params: nextParams })` and persist through row/meta conversion.

## Phase 4: Validation

### Step 9: Focused tests first
**Scope:** Medium
1. Run targeted tests around data modeling and rendering:
   ```sh
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.test.ts src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.test.tsx
   ```
2. Add tests for `sequence-drafts` insertion/replace helpers and `SequenceCreatorPanel` generation result handling.
3. Add Edge tests for `ai-generate-sequence` using the existing Edge test config.

### Step 10: Broader verification
**Scope:** Medium
1. Run relevant video editor tests:
   ```sh
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor
   ```
2. Run Edge function tests for the new endpoint:
   ```sh
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.edge.config.ts supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence
   ```
3. Finish with typecheck/build or the repo’s standard quality gate if runtime permits:
   ```sh
   npm run build
   ```

## Execution Order
1. Land data-model preservation before UI work; otherwise drafts will appear to work but lose `params` on save/edit.
2. Fix themed rendering before building preview; otherwise the panel preview cannot be trusted.
3. Add generator validation before connecting the UI generate button.
4. Add insertion helpers before panel actions so insert/replace are covered by pure tests.
5. Add the floating tool last, after the panel can generate, preview, and insert.

## Validation Order
1. Cheap unit tests for row/meta serialization and sequence draft validation.
2. Remotion renderer tests for registered sequence clip rendering.
3. Component tests for panel generate/edit/insert/replace behavior.
4. Edge function tests for structured AI output validation.
5. Video editor focused suite and build/typecheck.

      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-041",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-055",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-070",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-076",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content.",

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.",

      "id": "DEBT-089",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",

        "id": "DEBT-041",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately.",

        "id": "DEBT-055",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
        ]
      },

        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

      {
        "id": "DEBT-089",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 claims `updatepinnedgroupwithchildren` is 'currently imported by useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:357'. verified at useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:71-130 that this function is actually **defined locally** in useswitchtofinalvideo.ts, not imported from pinned-group-projection.ts. the real imports from pinned-group-projection at :5-8 are `buildgroupfromchildren` and `replacegroupchildren`, both of which are already in step 5's delete list. the plan's characterization is factually wrong (delete list shouldn't include updatepinnedgroupwithchildren as a pinned-group-projection export), but the substantive intent still works: once `buildgroupfromchildren`/`replacegroupchildren` are deleted, the local helper won't compile and step 11.2's 'inline soft-tag construction' rewrite removes it. minor wording error, not a blocking issue.",

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
Implement a professional AI animated sequence creator in the Reigh video editor. Add a Sequence tool beside the existing floating Text and Effect Layer tools. It should open a SequenceCreatorPanel for generating structured Remotion timeline sequence drafts from a user prompt plus optional selected/attached timeline assets. It should treat effects as wrappers and sequences as first-class timeline clips. It should use TimelineConfig.theme/theme_overrides when present but style/theme must be optional. It should generate trusted structured clipType+params drafts against existing registered themed sequence components first, preview them in Remotion, allow editing core params/timing/assets, then insert or replace a timeline clip. Store inserted sequences as normal timeline clips with clip.params and clip.hold; do not introduce shot-theme as source of truth. Reuse existing selected media/attachment patterns where practical, use existing timeline asset registry refs, add tests for insertion/data modeling/render path, and avoid arbitrary generated code by default.

User notes and answers:
- Hard constraint from user: try to use existing elements whenever possible. Reuse existing editor controls, selected-media/attachment chips, Remotion preview/player plumbing, resources/settings infrastructure, timeline mutation helpers, and current theme/registry patterns before introducing new UI or data shapes.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Professional AI Sequence Creator

## Overview
The prior plan was aimed at the right feature, but the critique exposed several root-cause gaps that would make a renderer-only or panel-only implementation fail. The revised approach keeps the original direction, but makes the foundation explicit: preserve sequence fields through timeline row/meta conversion, carry `theme` and `theme_overrides` through `ResolvedTimelineConfig`, provide a real installed-theme metadata source for Remotion, validate asset-valued params against existing registry/selected assets, and constrain sequence insertion/replacement to visual tracks.

The implementation should reuse existing editor pieces first: timeline mutation helpers, selected media/attachment hooks, existing dialog/control primitives, `RemotionPreview`/`TimelineRenderer` plumbing, current `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY` component dispatch, `ThemeChip`/theme patterns, and Edge function auth/rate-limit conventions. Effects remain wrapper clips (`effect-layer`); sequences are ordinary first-class clips with trusted `clipType`, `params`, and `hold`. No shot-theme source of truth and no arbitrary generated code are introduced.

## Phase 1: Timeline Data And Theme Foundation

### Step 1: Preserve sequence fields in timeline row/meta conversion (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Extend `ClipMeta` and `PinnedShotImageClipSnapshot.meta` to include schema-lift fields already supported by `TimelineClip`, especially `params`, `pool_id`, `clip_order`, `source_uuid`, and `generation` where relevant.
2. Update `getDefaultClipMeta()` and `rowsToConfig()` so sequence clips keep `clipType`, `hold`, and `params` through editor edits, save/load, undo/redo, and serialization.
3. Add a focused row/meta round-trip test proving `{ clipType: 'section-hook', hold: 3, params: { title: 'X' } }` survives `buildTimelineData()` and `rowsToConfig()`.

### Step 2: Carry theme data through resolved config (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts`, resolved-config fixtures/tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `ResolvedTimelineConfig` to include optional `theme`, `theme_overrides`, and `generation_defaults` copied from `TimelineConfig`.
2. Update `resolveTimelineConfig()` and any local resolved-config test helpers so preview/render callers receive the same top-level theme fields as the persisted timeline.
3. Add tests in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.test.ts` or adjacent fixtures proving resolved configs retain theme fields while timelines without theme fields remain valid.

### Step 3: Resolve installed themes for Remotion (`src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/sequences/themeRegistry.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Repair any existing syntax issue in `TimelineRenderer.tsx` before modifying the renderer, because this file is on the compile path for sequence preview and render tests.
2. Add a small browser-safe installed theme registry that maps known installed theme ids to full `Theme`-shaped metadata, starting with `2rp` from `@banodoco/timeline-theme-2rp` metadata. Keep fallback to `DEFAULT_THEME` from `@banodoco/timeline-composition/theme-api` when no theme is set.
3. Resolve `ResolvedTimelineConfig.theme` and `theme_overrides` into a full composition `Theme` value; do not rely on shot data or a loose untyped object without ensuring the `ThemeProvider` shape is complete.
4. Wrap registered themed sequence rendering with `ThemeProvider` from `@banodoco/timeline-composition/theme-api`, and continue using `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY` for component dispatch.
5. Add/expand `TimelineRenderer` tests for: registered clip renders without placeholder, params are passed through, theme overrides affect the provided theme, and missing theme fields fall back cleanly.

## Phase 2: Shared Trusted Sequence Metadata And Validation

### Step 4: Add Deno/browser-safe sequence metadata (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/metadata.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Create pure TypeScript metadata with no React, TSX component imports, or browser-only dependencies so both frontend code and Supabase Edge functions can import it.
2. Define the first trusted sequence set from installed 2rp clip types: `section-hook`, `art-card`, `resource-card`, and `cta-card`.
3. Include editable field metadata for core params used by existing components: strings such as `title`, `subtitle`, `kicker`, `caption`, `credit`, `label`, `detail`, `action`, `note`; string-list animation refs such as `entrance`/`exit`; and asset-list fields such as `previewAssetKeys` for `resource-card`.
4. In frontend-only code, cross-check this metadata against `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY` so a sequence type is offered only when its component is actually registered.

### Step 5: Add strict draft and asset validation (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Validate generated drafts against shared metadata: allowed `clipType`, positive finite `hold`, known params only, JSON-serializable scalar/list values, and no function/code/import/stringified JSX fields.
2. Treat asset-valued params specially. The model may output only asset keys from the supplied timeline registry/selected attachments, not arbitrary URLs.
3. Store registry-backed asset refs in `clip.params` as keys, for example `previewAssetKeys`, and materialize component-facing URLs only at preview/render time from `ResolvedTimelineConfig.registry`.
4. For existing components that expect URL params, such as `resource-card.params.previews`, add a renderer-side param materializer that derives `previews` from validated registry keys and never trusts model-supplied remote URLs.
5. Add tests covering unknown clip type rejection, arbitrary URL rejection in `previews`, allowed selected/registry asset key acceptance, and render-time URL materialization.

### Step 6: Add sequence generation Edge Function (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Mirror existing Edge auth, rate-limit, logging, and response conventions from `supabase/functions/ai-generate-effect/index.ts`.
2. Import only Deno-safe shared sequence metadata/validation or duplicate the exact metadata intentionally if import constraints require it; do not import `THEME_PACKAGE_REGISTRY` or TSX components from Edge code.
3. Request schema: `prompt`, optional compact `timeline`, optional `selected_clips`, optional `allowed_clip_types`, optional `allowed_assets`, optional `theme`, optional `theme_overrides`.
4. Response schema: `drafts: Array<{ clipType, params, hold, title?, assetRefs? }>`, optional `message`, `model`; no code fields.
5. Validate the model response server-side before returning it. Invalid drafts are dropped or returned as structured errors; arbitrary URLs and unknown params do not reach the client.
6. Add Edge tests for auth validation, bad prompt, unknown generated clip type rejection, arbitrary asset URL rejection, and valid structured draft output.

## Phase 3: Timeline Insertion And Editor Operations

### Step 7: Add sequence insertion/replace helpers (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/clip-editing/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Implement pure helpers to convert a validated sequence draft into the same row/meta mutation shape used by text and effect-layer insertion: `getNextClipId()`, `resolveOverlaps()`, `updateClipOrder()`, and `applyEdit({ type: 'rows' })`.
2. Insert sequences only on visual tracks. Insert defaults to the selected visual track, then the first visual track, creating a visual track only if the existing add-track helper already supports that path.
3. Replace-selected must not preserve an audio track. If the selected clip is on a visual track, preserve its `track` and `at`; if it is on an audio track, either disable replace in UI or relocate to the preferred visual fallback while preserving `at`.
4. Replacement removes the old clip from rows and clip order, inserts the sequence as a normal non-wrapper clip, and selects the new clip.
5. Add focused tests for insert-at-playhead, replace visual clip, reject/relocate audio-track replacement, overlap resolution, and persisted `params`/`hold`.

### Step 8: Add sequence inspector support using existing controls (`src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/ClipPanel.tsx`, small sequence param editor component)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Detect registered sequence clips through the sequence metadata/registry facade.
2. Show `timing` and sequence params editing for registered sequence clips instead of treating them like media clips.
3. Reuse existing controls first: `NumberInput`, `Input`, `Textarea`, `Select`, `ParameterControls` where the metadata maps cleanly, and existing asset/attachment display patterns for asset-key params.
4. Ensure updates call `onChange({ params: nextParams })` and persist through the row/meta changes from Phase 1.
5. Keep `ClipPanel` changes thin by moving sequence-specific param editing into a small dedicated component.

## Phase 4: Sequence Creator Panel And Tool Wiring

### Step 9: Build `SequenceCreatorPanel` with existing editor UI patterns (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Use existing dialog, textarea, button, input, select, slider/number-input, toast, and loading-state components rather than inventing a separate UI system.
2. Reuse `useSelectedMediaClips()` and `useTimelineClipsForAttachments()` for selected/attached asset context and summary chips; pass only registry-backed allowed assets to the Edge function.
3. Generate drafts through `ai-generate-sequence`, validate again client-side, and display a draft list with editable params, timing, and asset refs.
4. Preview the selected draft by composing a temporary normal sequence clip into the current resolved config and using existing Remotion preview/player plumbing around `TimelineRenderer`.
5. Provide explicit `Insert` and `Replace selected` actions. Disable or explain replace when the current selection cannot produce a visual-track replacement.
6. Keep arbitrary code generation out of the default flow.

### Step 10: Add the floating Sequence tool and thread open-state callbacks (`src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/VideoEditorShell.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a third floating button beside the existing Text and Effect Layer tools in `TimelineCanvas.tsx`, using a lucide icon and title/tooltip consistent with the current tool buttons.
2. Thread an `onOpenSequenceCreator` callback from `VideoEditorShell` through `TimelineEditor.tsx` into `TimelineCanvas.tsx`; do not hide the panel state in the canvas.
3. Preserve current Text and Effect Layer drag behavior unchanged.
4. Optionally pass the playhead/current time into the panel for insert defaults; keep drag-to-sequence insertion out of v1 unless it can reuse the existing drop-position path cleanly.

## Phase 5: Validation

### Step 11: Focused tests first
**Scope:** Medium
1. Run row/meta and serialization tests:
   ```sh
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.test.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.test.ts
   ```
2. Run renderer and sequence validation tests:
   ```sh
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/sequences
   ```
3. Run insertion helper tests for visual-track placement, replace behavior, asset ref validation, and params persistence.

### Step 12: Broader verification
**Scope:** Medium
1. Run the focused video editor suite:
   ```sh
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor
   ```
2. Run the new Edge tests:
   ```sh
   npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.edge.config.ts supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence
   ```
3. Finish with build/typecheck when the focused tests are stable:
   ```sh
   npm run build
   ```

## Execution Order
1. Fix compile-blocking renderer syntax before expanding renderer behavior.
2. Preserve `params` and carry theme fields through resolved config before building preview or insertion UI.
3. Add shared metadata and validation before connecting any AI generation.
4. Add pure insertion/replace helpers before panel actions.
5. Add inspector support and panel using existing controls.
6. Wire the floating tool last after the panel and helpers are testable.

## Validation Order
1. Start with cheap pure tests for data modeling, config resolution, sequence metadata, validation, and insertion helpers.
2. Add renderer tests for ThemeProvider, registered clip rendering, fallback theme, and URL materialization from asset keys.
3. Add component tests for panel generation result handling and insert/replace actions.
4. Add Edge tests for structured generation and server-side validation.
5. Run the broader video editor suite and build/typecheck.

    "Sequence metadata is pure TypeScript and shared by frontend and Edge where possible; if Edge import constraints appear, the same metadata is intentionally duplicated with tests to prevent drift.",
    "Sequence drafts store registry-backed asset keys in `clip.params`; renderer-side materialization converts those keys to component-facing URLs from `ResolvedTimelineConfig.registry`.",
    "Insert defaults to the selected visual track, then the first visual track. Replace preserves `at`; it preserves `track` only when the selected clip is already on a visual track.",
    "The new generator endpoint mirrors existing Edge auth/rate-limit conventions and does not persist timeline changes itself.",
    "No database migration is required because timeline JSON already supports open `clipType`, `params`, and `hold`."

  "signals": {
    "iteration": 2,
    "idea": "Implement a professional AI animated sequence creator in the Reigh video editor. Add a Sequence tool beside the existing floating Text and Effect Layer tools. It should open a SequenceCreatorPanel for generating structured Remotion timeline sequence drafts from a user prompt plus optional selected/attached timeline assets. It should treat effects as wrappers and sequences as first-class timeline clips. It should use TimelineConfig.theme/theme_overrides when present but style/theme must be optional. It should generate trusted structured clipType+params drafts against existing registered themed sequence components first, preview them in Remotion, allow editing core params/timing/assets, then insert or replace a timeline clip. Store inserted sequences as normal timeline clips with clip.params and clip.hold; do not introduce shot-theme as source of truth. Reuse existing selected media/attachment patterns where practical, use existing timeline asset registry refs, add tests for insertion/data modeling/render path, and avoid arbitrary generated code by default.",

      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-041",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-055",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-070",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-video-and-audio-clip-20260406-0616"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-076",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content.",

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",

        "id": "DEBT-041",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately.",

        "id": "DEBT-055",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
        ]
      },

        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

      {
        "id": "DEBT-089",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 claims `updatepinnedgroupwithchildren` is 'currently imported by useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:357'. verified at useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:71-130 that this function is actually **defined locally** in useswitchtofinalvideo.ts, not imported from pinned-group-projection.ts. the real imports from pinned-group-projection at :5-8 are `buildgroupfromchildren` and `replacegroupchildren`, both of which are already in step 5's delete list. the plan's characterization is factually wrong (delete list shouldn't include updatepinnedgroupwithchildren as a pinned-group-projection export), but the substantive intent still works: once `buildgroupfromchildren`/`replacegroupchildren` are deleted, the local helper won't compile and step 11.2's 'inline soft-tag construction' rewrite removes it. minor wording error, not a blocking issue.",

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
